<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the approximation-paving conjecture of Popa and Vaes for every maximal abelian subalgebra of a complex von Neumann algebra. For each $`0\lt \varepsilon\lt 1`$, every self-adjoint operator is a strong limit of self-adjoint operators of norm at most three times its norm, each admitting a norm paving with error at most <i>ε</i> times the approximant's own norm. The number of projections is at most $`C\varepsilon^{-6}`$ for a universal constant <i>C</i>. No separability or conditional-expectation hypothesis is required.

- **claim-main-approximation-paving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a universal constant $C_{\mathrm{ap}}>0$ such that for every $0<\varepsilon<1$ there is an integer $R(\varepsilon)\leq C_{\mathrm{ap}}\varepsilon^{-6}$ with the following property. Let $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a complex von Neumann algebra, let $A\subseteq M$ be a MASA, and let $x=x^*\in M$. For every finite $F\subset H$ and every $\delta>0$, there exist $y=y^*\in M$, a partition $\mathcal P=(p_i)_{i=1}^{R(\varepsilon)}$ of the unit in $A$, and $a=a^*\in A$, such that $$\left\lVert y\right\rVert\leq3\left\lVert x\right\rVert,\qquad \left\lVert(y-x)\xi\right\rVert<\delta\quad(\xi\in F),$$ and $$\left\lVert a\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert y\right\rVert,\qquad \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}(y)-a\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon\left\lVert y\right\rVert.$$ Zero projections may be included in the partition.
  - Explanation: Every self-adjoint operator over any MASA of a von Neumann algebra is a strong limit of bounded self-adjoint operators. Each of these is norm-paved with relative error epsilon by a number of projections depending only on epsilon.
- **claim-so-paving-expected** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-so-paving)): Suppose that a MASA $A\subseteq M$ is the range of a normal conditional expectation. For each $0<\varepsilon<1$ there is an integer $R_s(\varepsilon)$, independent of the inclusion, such that for every $x=x^*\in M$ and every strong-operator neighborhood $\mathcal V$ of zero, there exist an $R_s(\varepsilon)$-partition $\mathcal P$ in $A$, an $a=a^*\in A$ with $\left\lVert a\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert$, and a projection $q\in M$ with $\mathbf 1-q\in\mathcal V$, satisfying $$\left\lVert q(C_{\mathcal P}(x)-a)q\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon\left\lVert x\right\rVert.$$
  - Explanation: When the MASA is the range of a normal conditional expectation, strong-operator paving of the original operator holds with a partition size independent of the inclusion.
- **claim-quadratic-expected-approximation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-quadratic-expected-approximation)): There is a universal constant $C>0$ such that for every $0<\varepsilon<1$ one can choose an integer $N(\varepsilon)\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}$ with the following property. If the MASA $A\subseteq M$ is the range of a normal conditional expectation, then the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> holds with $N(\varepsilon)$ in place of $R(\varepsilon)$.
  - Explanation: With a normal conditional expectation onto the MASA, the main theorem holds with a partition count that is quadratic in the inverse error.
- **claim-compression-net** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compression-net)): Every $A$-partition compression $C_{\mathcal Q}$ preserves $\varphi$ and is contractive for both state seminorms. As $\mathcal Q$ refines through all finite $A$-partitions, $$C_{\mathcal Q}(z)\longrightarrow E_A(z) \quad\text{in }\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_\varphi^\# \qquad(z\in M).$$ The same holds along any refining sequence of partitions generating $A$.
  - Explanation: Partition compressions preserve the state, contract both state seminorms, and converge to the conditional expectation as partitions refine.
- **claim-sequence-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sequence-quotient)): Let $$\mathcal I_\varphi= \{(z_j)\in\ell^\infty(M):\left\lVert z_j\right\rVert_\varphi^\#\to0\},$$ and let $$\mathcal C_\varphi= \{t\in\ell^\infty(M): t\mathcal I_\varphi\subseteq\mathcal I_\varphi,\ \mathcal I_\varphi t\subseteq\mathcal I_\varphi\}.$$ Then $\mathcal C_\varphi$ is a unital $C^*$-algebra and $\mathcal I_\varphi$ is a closed two-sided ideal in it. Constant sequences from $M$ and all sequences in $\ell^\infty(A)$ belong to $\mathcal C_\varphi$.
  - Explanation: The bounded sequences multiplying the state-null ideal form a unital C*-algebra in which that ideal is a closed two-sided ideal; constant and abelian-subalgebra sequences belong to it.
- **claim-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stability)): Put $t_i^{1/2}=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\sqrt{t_{vi}})_{v\in Q}$. Then $$\label{poly:differential-formula} f_Q(Y)= \left(\prod_{v\in Q}\frac{1}{r!}\partial_{Y_v}^{r-1}\right) \prod_{i=1}^r \det\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(Y+a)-r t_i^{1/2}Wt_i^{1/2}\bigr).$$ This identity remains valid for complex $t_{vi}$ with $\sum_i t_{vi}=1$, where the expectation means the finite algebraic sum with the corresponding product weights, and the same square root is used in both occurrences of $t_i^{1/2}$. The algebraic identity also holds with complex penalties and arbitrary complex off-diagonal entries of $W$, still with zero diagonal.* *More generally, let $\mathcal H$ be an open half-plane. If the product of determinants on the right of <a href="#poly:differential-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="poly:differential-formula">[poly:differential-formula]</a>, for every principal restriction, is nonzero whenever all its variables lie in $\mathcal H$, then the same holds for $f_Q$. In particular, for Hermitian $W$ and real probabilities and penalties, $f_Q$ is upper-half-plane stable and its univariate specialization is real-rooted.
  - Explanation: The expected determinant polynomial under random colorings has a differential representation, and for Hermitian data it is stable with real-rooted univariate specialization.
- **claim-local-spectral-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-data)): Fix $v\in Q$, put $f=f_Q$, $g=f_{Q\setminus\{v\}}$, and write $h=f/g$, $G=1/h$ at univariate arguments. For $z\in\mathbb C$ with $\operatorname{Im}z>0$, $$\label{poly:pick-inequality} \operatorname{Im}h(z)\geq\operatorname{Im}z.$$ If $s_j$ are the roots of $g$, with multiplicities, there are $\nu_j\geq0$ such that $$\label{poly:arrow-quotient} h(z)=z+a_v-\sum_j\frac{\nu_j}{z-s_j}.$$ Consequently, on choosing $\|c_j\|^2=\nu_j$, $$\label{poly:arrow-matrix} B=\begin{pmatrix}-a_v&c^*\\ c&S\end{pmatrix}, \qquad S=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(s_j), \qquad \det(zI-B)=f(z).$$ The function $G$ is the transform $$\label{poly:stieltjes} G(z)=\int_\mathbb R\frac{d\gamma_v(x)}{z-x}$$ of the spectral probability measure of $B$ at its first coordinate. Its first two moments are $$\label{poly:moments} \begin{gathered} \int x\,d\gamma_v(x)=-a_v, \qquad \int x^2\,d\gamma_v(x)=a_v^2+\sum_j\nu_j, \\ \sum_j\nu_j=\sum_{w\ne v}\|W_{vw}\|^2\sum_i t_{vi}t_{wi}. \end{gathered}$$* *Let $N_f(x)$ and $N_g(x)$ count roots strictly greater than $x$, including multiplicities, and define $$\label{poly:local-functionals} \xi_h(x)=N_f(x)-N_g(x),\qquad \ell_v=\int_0^\infty\xi_h(x)\,dx,\qquad m_v=\int_\mathbb Rx_+\,d\gamma_v(x).$$ Then $\xi_h=\mathbf1_{\{h<0\}}$ off a finite subset of $\mathbb R$, and $$\label{poly:poisson} \frac{1}{\pi}\arg h(x+i\eta) =(P_\eta*\xi_h)(x), \qquad P_\eta(x)=\frac{\eta}{\pi(x^2+\eta^2)},\quad\eta>0,$$ where $\arg h\in(0,\pi)$. Moreover, $$\label{poly:sum-local-measures} \sum_{v\in Q}G_{v,Q}(z)=\frac{f'_Q(z)}{f_Q(z)}, \qquad \sum_{v\in Q}\gamma_v=\sum_{f_Q(\lambda)=0}\delta_\lambda,$$ with multiplicities on the right.* *If $v$ is made deterministic of color $i$, write $h_i$ for the new quotient relative to the same $g$, and put $R_i=h_i/h$. Its representation has the same $a_v$ and poles $s_j$, and masses $\nu_{ij}\geq0$ which can be chosen so that $$\label{poly:pin-average} h=\sum_i t_{vi}h_i,\qquad \nu_j=\sum_i t_{vi}\nu_{ij}.$$ If the penalty at $v$ is also changed to $Ka_v$, the quotient becomes $$\label{poly:buffered-quotient} h_i'=h_i+(K-1)a_v.$$ Here the prime with a color index denotes the buffered quotient, not a derivative. We write $\ell'_{v,i}$ for the positive-axis integral of the spectral shift defined using $h_i'$ and the unchanged $g$.
  - Explanation: The quotient of a polynomial by its vertex-deleted polynomial is a Pick-type function with an arrow-matrix representation, a spectral probability measure with explicit first two moments, and a spectral-shift function linked by a Poisson identity.
- **claim-initial-largest-root** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-paving)): Let $T$ be a Hermitian contraction on a nonempty finite index set $Q$, with zero diagonal. In <a href="#poly:definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="poly:definition">[poly:definition]</a>, take $W=T$, $a_v=0$, and $t_{vi}=1/r$. Then $$\label{poly:mss-bound} \max\operatorname{root}(f_Q)\leq2\sqrt{2/r}+2/r.$$ In particular, giving every vertex the same penalty $b$ places all roots strictly below zero if $2\sqrt{2/r}+2/r<b$.
  - Explanation: For a zero-diagonal Hermitian contraction under uniform random coloring, the largest root of the expected polynomial is bounded explicitly in the number of colors.
- **claim-residue-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-poly-tail-control)): Suppose $a=1$ in <a href="#poly:general-arrow" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="poly:general-arrow">[poly:general-arrow]</a>, and set $H=\int\|x\|\,d\gamma(x)=1+2m$. There is a universal $C_0$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \sum_{\|s_j\|\leq60H}\nu_j&\leq C_0H^2, \label{poly:near-mass}\\ \sum_{s_j\geq20H}\frac{\nu_j}{s_j}&\leq C_0m, \label{poly:positive-tail}\\ \sum_{s_j\leq-20H}\frac{\nu_j}{\|s_j\|}&\leq C_0H. \label{poly:negative-tail} \end{aligned}$$ For example, $C_0=6000$ suffices.
  - Explanation: Near mass and positive and negative tails of the residues of an arrow quotient are controlled by universal multiples of the first moments.
- **claim-boole-length** (theorem, [source](#stmt-poly-boole)): For finitely many real poles $s_j$, nonnegative weights $w_j$, and $c>0$, $$\label{poly:boole-identities} \left\|\left\{\lambda\in\mathbb R: \sum_j\frac{w_j}{\lambda-s_j}>c\right\}\right\| =\left\|\left\{\lambda\in\mathbb R: \sum_j\frac{w_j}{\lambda-s_j}<-c\right\}\right\| =\frac{\sum_jw_j}{c}.$$ If every pole carrying positive weight is strictly positive, the first exceedance set lies in $(0,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: Boole's identity gives the exact Lebesgue measure of each exceedance set of a weighted sum of simple poles as total weight over level.
- **claim-buffered-pin** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-buffered-pin)): There are universal constants $K\geq1$ and $C>0$ with the following property. In <a href="#poly:general-arrow" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="poly:general-arrow">[poly:general-arrow]</a>, let a finite family of choices have masses $\nu_{ij}\geq0$ and probabilities $t_i\geq0$ such that $\sum_i t_i=1$ and $\sum_i t_i\nu_{ij}=\nu_j$. Keep the poles $s_j$ unchanged, and define $$h_i'(z)=z+Ka-\sum_j\frac{\nu_{ij}}{z-s_j}, \qquad \ell_i'=L(Ka,\nu_i),\qquad \ell=L(a,\nu).$$ Then $$\label{poly:buffered-pin-bound} \min_{i:t_i>0}\ell_i'\leq\ell+Cm.$$ The constants have no dependence on the number or positions of the poles, the masses, the probabilities, or the positive penalty $a$.
  - Explanation: Raising the penalty by a universal factor lets one choose a color whose exceedance length exceeds the parent length by at most a universal multiple of the positive moment.
- **claim-measured-paving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-measured-paving)): There is a universal constant $K\geq1$ with the following property. Suppose $T$, $\mathcal R$, $\mathcal G$, and $\omega$ satisfy the preceding assumptions. Let $b>0$, and choose a positive integer $r$ such that $$2\sqrt{2/r}+2/r<b. \label{slab:r-choice}$$ For every $\sigma>0$ and $\rho>0$, there is a measurable coloring of $V$ with $r+1$ colors for which $$\int_V \bigl((T_{\mathrm{pav}}-(Kb+\sigma)\mathbf 1)_+^2\bigr)_{vv} \,d\mu(v)<\rho. \label{slab:measured-conclusion}$$
  - Explanation: For a bounded-degree measured graph system on a nonsingular relation, a measurable coloring with one extra color makes the integrated squared positive spectral excess arbitrarily small.
- **claim-slab-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slab-limits)): On each bounded slab, the finite local functions $h_u,G_u,h_{u,i},h_{u,i}',R_{u,i}$, the probability and residue measures, and the data $m_u,\ell_u,\ell_{u,i}'$ have unique limits as finite graph balls increase to a component. They are measurable and independent of the exhaustion, and the local Cauchy-transform and Poisson-transform identities pass to the limits. Their supports have a common fixed-slab bound, and Equation <a href="#slab:local-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="slab:local-bounds">[slab:local-bounds]</a> continues to hold.* *The Laurent coefficients through order $k$ of $h_u/z$, $zG_u$, $h_{u,i}/z$, $h_{u,i}'/z$, and $R_{u,i}$, and the moments of $\gamma_u$ through order $k$, depend only on the data within graph distance $k$ of $u$. The same assertion holds for coefficients of logarithmic derivatives under data perturbations, with the local perturbation directions included in the data. It remains valid under arbitrary deletion configurations.* *At an unassigned vertex the buffered pin inequality of Lemma <a href="#lem:buffered-pin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:buffered-pin">11</a> holds for these infinite local data. If every base vertex is unassigned, then $m_u=0$ on every bounded slab.
  - Explanation: On a bounded slab the local spectral data have unique measurable limits as finite balls exhaust a component, depend only on bounded-radius data at each order, and vanish when all vertices are unassigned.
- **claim-far-deletion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-far-deletion)): Consider any bounded slab containing $V\times[0,1]$, any base partial assignment, and any further measurable restriction that retains the central vertices being tested. Deleting a measurable set of vertices at heights $s>R$, or at heights $s<-R$, changes the tuple in Equation <a href="#slab:normalized-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="slab:normalized-data">[slab:normalized-data]</a> by an amount tending to zero in $L^1(d\mu\,ds)$ on the central unassigned band as $R\to\infty$. The convergence is uniform in the slab, the partial assignment, and the deletion set. The same conclusion holds for the union of a far high set and a far low set.
  - Explanation: Deleting vertices far above or far below the central band changes the normalized spectral data by an amount vanishing in integral norm, uniformly in the slab, assignment and deletion set.
- **claim-coherent-choice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coherent-choice)): For every $\tau_0>0$ there are arbitrarily large integers $L\geq1$ with the following property, uniformly over all base partial assignments. On the slab $V\times[0,L]$ one can choose a measurable prospective color $i(v)$ at each unassigned base vertex, using the same color at every height, so that $$\frac1L\int_{\mathrm{unassigned}\ \mathrm{in}\ V\times[0,L]} (\ell_{(v,s),i(v)}'-\ell_{(v,s)})\,d\widetilde\mu(v,s) \leq C\Phi+\tau_0, \qquad \Phi=\frac1L\int_{V\times[0,L]}m_{(v,s)}\,d\widetilde\mu(v,s). \label{slab:coherent-inequality}$$ Here $C$ is the universal constant in Lemma <a href="#lem:buffered-pin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:buffered-pin">11</a>, and every $\ell_{u,i}'$ on the left is the insertion cost after changing only the lifted vertex $u$. The same $L$ can be used at every stage of a sequence of partial assignments.
  - Explanation: A single prospective color per base vertex, used at every height, achieves the buffered insertion-cost bound on a long slab up to a tolerance.
- **claim-sparse-pinning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sparse-pinning)): For every $\zeta,\eta>0$ and $L_0\geq1$, there are an integer $L\geq L_0$ and a measurable partition $$V=\mathcal U\sqcup V_1\sqcup\cdots\sqcup V_r$$ such that $\mu(\mathcal U)<\eta$ and the following holds. On the surviving lifted vertices $(V\setminus\mathcal U)\times[0,L]$, let $T_{\mathrm{col}}^L$ be the restriction of $T$, keeping only entries between vertices whose basepoints belong to the same $V_i$, and put $$\label{pin:surviving-operator} A_L=d^{1/2}(T_{\mathrm{col}}^L-Kb\mathbf 1)d^{1/2}, \qquad d_{(v,s)}=e^s.$$ Extend $m_u^{\mathrm{surv}}=((A_L)_+)_{uu}$ by zero on the deleted vertices. Then $$\label{pin:surviving-cost} \frac1L\int_{V\times[0,L]}m_u^{\mathrm{surv}}\, d\widetilde\mu(u)<\zeta.$$
  - Explanation: Iterated sparse random pinning yields a partial coloring whose unassigned set is small and whose surviving average positive cost on a long slab is small.
- **claim-descent** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-descent)): Fix $\sigma,\beta>0$, and choose a real polynomial $p$ of degree at most $k\geq1$ such that $$\sup_{x\in[-1,1]} \left\|p(x)-(x-(Kb+\sigma))_+^2\right\|\leq\beta.$$ Suppose a base partial assignment, with unassigned set $\mathcal U$, and a slab length $L\geq\max\{1,4k\log J\}$ have surviving cost $$\Psi=\frac1L\int_{V\times[0,L]}m_u^{\mathrm{surv}}\, d\widetilde\mu(u),$$ where $m^{\mathrm{surv}}$ is defined from <a href="#pin:surviving-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pin:surviving-operator">[pin:surviving-operator]</a>. Complete the base coloring by giving $\mathcal U$ one additional color, and denote its paved matrix system by $T_{\mathrm{pav}}$. Then $$\label{pin:descent-bound} \int_V\bigl((T_{\mathrm{pav}}-(Kb+\sigma)\mathbf 1)_+^2\bigr)_{vv} \,d\mu(v) \leq2\beta+\mu(\mathcal U)+\frac{2J^kN_k}{\sigma}\Psi.$$
  - Explanation: A small surviving slab cost transfers to the base: with one extra color on the unassigned set, the integrated squared spectral excess is bounded by explicit terms.
- **claim-state-paving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-state-paving)): For every $\gamma>0$ there is an integer $R_{\mathrm{st}}(\gamma)$ with the following property. For every self-adjoint $x\in M$ with $\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq1$, there are partitions $\mathcal P_j$ of $1$ in $A$, each with $R_{\mathrm{st}}(\gamma)$ members, and self-adjoint $w_j\in M$ such that $$\label{exp:state-paving} \left\lVert w_j\right\rVert\leq2,\qquad \left\lVert w_j\right\rVert_\varphi\longrightarrow0,\qquad \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P_j}(x-w_j)-E_A(x)\right\rVert\leq\gamma.$$ The integer $R_{\mathrm{st}}(\gamma)$ is independent of $M,A,\varphi$, and $x$.
  - Explanation: In the state-preserving separable setting, every self-adjoint contraction is paved up to a state-null correction using a partition size independent of the algebra, MASA and state.
- **claim-normalizer-paving** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-normalizer-paving)): Let $b,\sigma>0$ and let $r$ be an integer such that $$2\sqrt{2/r}+2/r<b.$$ There are sequences of $A$-partitions with $(r+1)^2$ members whose quotient partition $\mathcal P$ satisfies $$\label{exp:normalizer-bound} \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}[Y]\right\rVert\leq6(Kb+\sigma),$$ where $K$ is the universal constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:measured-paving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:measured-paving">12</a>.
  - Explanation: The normalizer part of the operator is paved in the quotient by a bounded-size partition with norm controlled by the measured paving constant.
- **claim-off-normalizer** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-off-normalizer)): For every $n\geq2$, there are sequences of $n$-partitions in $A$ whose quotient partition $\mathcal P$ satisfies $$\label{exp:off-normalizer-bound} \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}[Z]\right\rVert\leq\frac4{\sqrt n}.$$
  - Explanation: The part orthogonal to the normalizer algebra can be paved in the quotient with norm decaying like the inverse square root of the partition size.
- **claim-atomless-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-atomless)): If $E_N(X)=0$, then $k_X(v,\cdot)$ has no atoms for almost every $v\in V$.
  - Explanation: An operator whose normalizer-algebra expectation vanishes has an atomless joint spectral kernel almost everywhere.
- **claim-random-freeness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-random-freeness)): Let $\mathcal M$ have separable predual, let $\phi_0$ be a faithful normal state, and let $D\subset\mathcal M_{\phi_0}$ be a diffuse MASA. Suppose that $U\in\mathcal M$ is a self-adjoint unitary with $\phi_0(U)=0$, and that the normal positive map $$\mathcal K(a)=E_D(UaU),\qquad a\in D,$$ has an almost everywhere diffuse kernel on a standard probability model of $(D,\phi_0\|_D)$. For every integer $n\geq2$ there are unitaries $w_l\in D$, with $w_l^n=1$, such that the joint star moments of $(w_l,U)$ converge to those of free unitaries $(w,U_{\mathrm f})$, where $w$ is uniform on the $n$th roots of unity and $U_{\mathrm f}$ is uniform on $\{-1,1\}$.
  - Explanation: Under a diffuse kernel, random diagonal unitaries of finite order are asymptotically free from the self-adjoint unitary, even for a nontracial faithful normal state.
- **claim-free-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-free-compression)): Let $w$ and $U_{\mathrm f}$ have the free law in Lemma <a href="#lem:random-freeness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:random-freeness">22</a>, in its reduced free-product representation. If $p_1,\ldots,p_n$ are the spectral projections of $w$, then $$\label{exp:free-norm-bound} \left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^n p_j U_{\mathrm f}p_j\right\rVert\leq\frac2{\sqrt n}.$$
  - Explanation: In the free model, compressing the symmetry by the spectral projections of the uniform root-of-unity unitary gives an operator of small norm.
- **claim-separable-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-separable-reduction)): The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:state-paving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:state-paving">18</a> holds without the assumption of separable predual, with the same partition-size bound.
  - Explanation: The state-preserving paving theorem extends to non-separable predual with the same partition-size bound, by a countably generated submodel.
- **claim-no-central-functional** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-central-functional)): Let $D\subset P$ be a MASA such that no nonzero normal positive functional on $P$ centralizes $D$. For every normal state $\theta$ on $P$, every integer $m\geq2$, and every $\alpha>0$, there are a positive contraction $s\in P$ and an $m$-partition $(p_i)_{i=1}^m$ in $D$ such that $$\label{gen:small-diagonal} \theta(s)>1-\alpha,\qquad p_i s p_i\leq m^{-1}p_i \quad(1\leq i\leq m).$$
  - Explanation: If no normal positive functional centralizes the MASA, one finds positive contractions carrying almost full mass with small compressions on each cell of a partition.
- **claim-no-central-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gen-no-central-approximation)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:no-central-functional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:no-central-functional">25</a>, let $P$ act normally on a Hilbert space and let $x=x^*\in P$ satisfy $\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq1$. For each $m\geq2$, the operators $sxs$ supplied by that lemma approximate $x$ strongly, with associated $m$-partitions $\mathcal P$ satisfying $$\left\lVert sxs\right\rVert\leq1,\qquad \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}(sxs)\right\rVert\leq1/m.$$ Here the approximation is on any prescribed finite set of vectors, to any positive accuracy, while $m$ stays fixed.
  - Explanation: Without centralizing functionals, the operators obtained by sandwiching with the positive contraction approximate the given operator strongly and have small paved norm.
- **claim-support-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gen-support-decomposition)): For any MASA $A\subset M$, set $$e=\bigvee\{\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\lambda):\lambda\in M_*^+, \ \lambda\text{ centralizes }A\},\qquad h=\mathbf 1-e.$$ Then $e,h\in A$. If $e\ne0$, there is a faithful normal conditional expectation $E:eMe\to Ae$. If $h\ne0$, the inclusion $Ah\subset hMh$ has no nonzero centralizing normal positive functional.
  - Explanation: The unit splits into a projection of the MASA carrying a faithful normal expectation onto the MASA corner and a complement with no centralizing normal functional.

        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C>0$ such that for every $0<\\varepsilon<1$ one can choose an integer $N(\\varepsilon)\\leq C\\varepsilon^{-2}$ with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compression-net",
        "exact_quote": "Every $A$-partition compression $C_{\\mathcal Q}$ preserves $\\varphi$ and is contractive for both state seminorms.         "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sequence-quotient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$\\mathcal I_\\varphi=\n \\{(z_j)\\in\\ell^\\infty(M):\\left\\lVert z_j\\right\\rVert_\\varphi^\\#\\to0\\},$$ and let $$\\mathcal C_\\varphi=\n \\{t\\in\\ell^\\infty(M):\n t\\mathcal I_\\varphi\\subseteq\\mathcal I_\\varphi,\\\n \\mathcal I_\\varphi t\\subseteq\\mathcal I_\\varphi\\}.$$ Then $\\mathcal C_\\varphi$ is a unital $C^*$-algebra and $\\mathcal I_\\varphi$ is a closed two-sided ideal in it.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Put $t_i^{1/2}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(\\sqrt{t_{vi}})_{v\\in Q}$. Then $$\\label{poly:differential-formula}\n f_Q(Y)=\n \\left(\\prod_{v\\in Q}\\frac{1}{r!}\\partial_{Y_v}^{r-1}\\right)\n \\prod_{i=1}^r\n \\det\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(Y+a)-r t_i^{1/2}Wt_i^{1/2}\\bigr).$$ This identity remains valid for complex $t_{vi}$ with $\\sum_i t_{vi}=1$, where the expectation means the finite algebraic sum with the corresponding product weights, and the same square root is used in both occurrences of $t_i^{1/2}$. The algebraic identity also holds with complex penalties and arbitrary complex off-diagonal entries of $W$, still with zero diagonal.*\n\n*More generally, let $\\mathcal H$ be an open half-plane. If the product of determinants on the right of <a href=\"#poly:differential-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"poly:differential-formula\">[poly:differential-formula]</a>, for every principal restriction, is nonzero whenever all its variables lie in $\\mathcal H$, then the same holds for $f_Q$. In particular, for Hermitian $W$ and real probabilities and penalties, $f_Q$ is upper-half-plane stable and its univariate specialization is real-rooted.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability",
          "exact_quote": "Put $t_i^{1/2}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(\\sqrt{t_{vi}})_{v\\in Q}$. The algebraic identity also holds with complex penalties and arbitrary complex off-diagonal entries of $W$, still with zero diagonal.*\n\n*More generally, let $\\mathcal H$ be an open half-plane. If the product of determinants on the right of <a href=\"#poly:differential-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"poly:differential-formula\">[poly:differential-formula]</a>, for every principal restriction, is nonzero whenever all its variables lie in $\\mathcal H$, then the same holds for $f_Q$. In particular, for Hermitian $W$ and real probabilities and penalties, $f_Q$ is upper-half-plane stable and its univariate specialization is real-rooted.",
          "source_id": "PM-300.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $t_i^{1/2}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(\\sqrt{t_{vi}})_{v\\in Q}$. The algebraic identity also holds with complex penalties and arbitrary complex off-diagonal entries of $W$, still with zero diagonal.*\n\n*More generally, let $\\mathcal H$ be an open half-plane.       "explanation": "The expected determinant polynomial under random colorings has a differential representation, and for Hermitian data it is stable with real-rooted univariate specialization.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-data",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $v\\in Q$, put $f=f_Q$, $g=f_{Q\\setminus\\{v\\}}$, and write $h=f/g$, $G=1/h$ at univariate arguments. For $z\\in\\mathbb C$ with $\\operatorname{Im}z>0$, $$\\label{poly:pick-inequality}\n \\operatorname{Im}h(z)\\geq\\operatorname{Im}z.$$ If $s_j$ are the roots of $g$, with multiplicities, there are $\\nu_j\\geq0$ such that $$\\label{poly:arrow-quotient}\n h(z)=z+a_v-\\sum_j\\frac{\\nu_j}{z-s_j}.$$ Consequently, on choosing $|c_j|^2=\\nu_j$, $$\\label{poly:arrow-matrix}\n B=\\begin{pmatrix}-a_v&c^*\\\\ c&S\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(s_j),\n \\qquad \\det(zI-B)=f(z).$$ The function $G$ is the transform $$\\label{poly:stieltjes}\n G(z)=\\int_\\mathbb R\\frac{d\\gamma_v(x)}{z-x}$$ of the spectral probability measure of $B$ at its first coordinate. Its first two moments are $$\\label{poly:moments}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\int x\\,d\\gamma_v(x)=-a_v,\n \\qquad\n \\int x^2\\,d\\gamma_v(x)=a_v^2+\\sum_j\\nu_j,\n \\\\\n \\sum_j\\nu_j=\\sum_{w\\ne v}|W_{vw}|^2\\sum_i t_{vi}t_{wi}.\n \\end{gathered}$$*\n\n\n\n*Let $N_f(x)$ and $N_g(x)$ count roots strictly greater than $x$, including multiplicities, and define $$\\label{poly:local-functionals}\n \\xi_h(x)=N_f(x)-N_g(x),\\qquad\n \\ell_v=\\int_0^\\infty\\xi_h(x)\\,dx,\\qquad\n m_v=\\int_\\mathbb Rx_+\\,d\\gamma_v(x).$$ Then $\\xi_h=\\mathbf1_{\\{h<0\\}}$ off a finite subset of $\\mathbb R$, and $$\\label{poly:poisson}\n \\frac{1}{\\pi}\\arg h(x+i\\eta)\n   =(P_\\eta*\\xi_h)(x),\n \\qquad P_\\eta(x)=\\frac{\\eta}{\\pi(x^2+\\eta^2)},\\quad\\eta>0,$$ where $\\arg h\\in(0,\\pi)$. Moreover, $$\\label{poly:sum-local-measures}\n \\sum_{v\\in Q}G_{v,Q}(z)=\\frac{f'_Q(z)}{f_Q(z)},\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{v\\in Q}\\gamma_v=\\sum_{f_Q(\\lambda)=0}\\delta_\\lambda,$$ with multiplicities on the right.*\n\n\n\n*If $v$ is made deterministic of color $i$, write $h_i$ for the new quotient relative to the same $g$, and put $R_i=h_i/h$. Its representation has the same $a_v$ and poles $s_j$, and masses $\\nu_{ij}\\geq0$ which can be chosen so that $$\\label{poly:pin-average}\n h=\\sum_i t_{vi}h_i,\\qquad\n \\nu_j=\\sum_i t_{vi}\\nu_{ij}.$$ If the penalty at $v$ is also changed to $Ka_v$, the quotient becomes $$\\label{poly:buffered-quotient}\n h_i'=h_i+(K-1)a_v.$$ Here the prime with a color index denotes the buffered quotient, not a derivative. We write $\\ell'_{v,i}$ for the positive-axis integral of the spectral shift defined using $h_i'$ and the unchanged $g$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },

Its first two moments are $$\\label{poly:moments}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\int x\\,d\\gamma_v(x)=-a_v,\n \\qquad\n \\int x^2\\,d\\gamma_v(x)=a_v^2+\\sum_j\\nu_j,\n \\\\\n \\sum_j\\nu_j=\\sum_{w\\ne v}|W_{vw}|^2\\sum_i t_{vi}t_{wi}.\n \\end{gathered}$$*\n\n\n\n*Let $N_f(x)$ and $N_g(x)$ count roots strictly greater than $x$, including multiplicities, and define $$\\label{poly:local-functionals}\n \\xi_h(x)=N_f(x)-N_g(x),\\qquad\n \\ell_v=\\int_0^\\infty\\xi_h(x)\\,dx,\\qquad\n m_v=\\int_\\mathbb Rx_+\\,d\\gamma_v(x).$$ Then $\\xi_h=\\mathbf1_{\\{h<0\\}}$ off a finite subset of $\\mathbb R$, and $$\\label{poly:poisson}\n \\frac{1}{\\pi}\\arg h(x+i\\eta)\n   =(P_\\eta*\\xi_h)(x),\n \\qquad P_\\eta(x)=\\frac{\\eta}{\\pi(x^2+\\eta^2)},\\quad\\eta>0,$$ where $\\arg h\\in(0,\\pi)$. Moreover, $$\\label{poly:sum-local-measures}\n \\sum_{v\\in Q}G_{v,Q}(z)=\\frac{f'_Q(z)}{f_Q(z)},\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{v\\in Q}\\gamma_v=\\sum_{f_Q(\\lambda)=0}\\delta_\\lambda,$$ with multiplicities on the right.*\n\n\n\n*If $v$ is made deterministic of color $i$, write $h_i$ for the new quotient relative to the same $g$, and put $R_i=h_i/h$. We write $\\ell'_{v,i}$ for the positive-axis integral of the spectral shift defined using $h_i'$ and the unchanged $g$.",
      "explanation": "The quotient of a polynomial by its vertex-deleted polynomial is a Pick-type function with an arrow-matrix representation, a spectral probability measure with explicit first two moments, and a spectral-shift function linked by a Poisson identity.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mss-paving",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a Hermitian contraction on a nonempty finite index set $Q$, with zero diagonal. In <a href=\"#poly:definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"poly:definition\">[poly:definition]</a>, take $W=T$, $a_v=0$, and $t_{vi}=1/r$. Then $$\\label{poly:mss-bound}\n \\max\\operatorname{root}(f_Q)\\leq2\\sqrt{2/r}+2/r.$$ In particular, giving every vertex the same penalty $b$ places all roots strictly below zero if $2\\sqrt{2/r}+2/r<b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-300.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-poly-tail-control",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $a=1$ in <a href=\"#poly:general-arrow\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"poly:general-arrow\">[poly:general-arrow]</a>, and set $H=\\int|x|\\,d\\gamma(x)=1+2m$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-poly-boole",
        "exact_quote": "For finitely many real poles $s_j$, nonnegative weights $w_j$, and $c>0$, $$\\label{poly:boole-identities}\n \\left|\\left\\{\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R:\n       \\sum_j\\frac{w_j}{\\lambda-s_j}>c\\right\\}\\right|\n =\\left|\\left\\{\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R:\n       \\sum_j\\frac{w_j}{\\lambda-s_j}<-c\\right\\}\\right|\n =\\frac{\\sum_jw_j}{c}.$$ If every pole carrying positive weight is strictly positive, the first exceedance set lies in $(0,\\infty)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-poly-boole",
          "exact_quote": "For finitely many real poles $s_j$, nonnegative weights $w_j$, and $c>0$, $$\\label{poly:boole-identities}\n \\left|\\left\\{\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R:\n       \\sum_j\\frac{w_j}{\\lambda-s_j}>c\\right\\}\\right|\n =\\left|\\left\\{\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R:\n       \\sum_j\\frac{w_j}{\\lambda-s_j}<-c\\right\\}\\right|\n =\\frac{\\sum_jw_j}{c}.$$ If every pole carrying positive weight is strictly positive, the first exceedance set lies in $(0,\\infty)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-300.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For finitely many real poles $s_j$, nonnegative weights $w_j$, and $c>0$, $$\\label{poly:boole-identities}\n \\left|\\left\\{\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R:\n       \\sum_j\\frac{w_j}{\\lambda-s_j}>c\\right\\}\\right|\n =\\left|\\left\\{\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R:\n       \\sum_j\\frac{w_j}{\\lambda-s_j}<-c\\right\\}\\right|\n =\\frac{\\sum_jw_j}{c}.$$ If every pole carrying positive weight is strictly positive, the first exceedance set lies in $(0,\\infty)$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-buffered-pin",
        "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $K\\geq1$ and $C>0$ with the following property. In <a href=\"#poly:general-arrow\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"poly:general-arrow\">[poly:general-arrow]</a>, let a finite family of choices have masses $\\nu_{ij}\\geq0$ and probabilities $t_i\\geq0$ such that $\\sum_i t_i=1$ and $\\sum_i t_i\\nu_{ij}=\\nu_j$. Keep the poles $s_j$ unchanged, and define $$h_i'(z)=z+Ka-\\sum_j\\frac{\\nu_{ij}}{z-s_j},\n \\qquad \\ell_i'=L(Ka,\\nu_i),\\qquad \\ell=L(a,\\nu).$$ Then $$\\label{poly:buffered-pin-bound}\n \\min_{i:t_i>0}\\ell_i'\\leq\\ell+Cm.$$ The constants have no dependence on the number or positions of the poles, the masses, the probabilities, or the positive penalty $a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $K\\geq1$ with the following property. Suppose $T$, $\\mathcal R$, $\\mathcal G$, and $\\omega$ satisfy the preceding assumptions.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slab-limits",
        "exact_quote": "On each bounded slab, the finite local functions $h_u,G_u,h_{u,i},h_{u,i}',R_{u,i}$, the probability and residue measures, and the data $m_u,\\ell_u,\\ell_{u,i}'$ have unique limits as finite graph balls increase to a component. They are measurable and independent of the exhaustion, and the local Cauchy-transform and Poisson-transform identities pass to the limits. Their supports have a common fixed-slab bound, and Equation <a href=\"#slab:local-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"slab:local-bounds\">[slab:local-bounds]</a> continues to hold.*\n\n*The Laurent coefficients through order $k$ of $h_u/z$, $zG_u$, $h_{u,i}/z$, $h_{u,i}'/z$, and $R_{u,i}$, and the moments of $\\gamma_u$ through order $k$, depend only on the data within graph distance $k$ of $u$. The same assertion holds for coefficients of logarithmic derivatives under data perturbations, with the local perturbation directions included in the data. It remains valid under arbitrary deletion configurations.*\n\n*At an unassigned vertex the buffered pin inequality of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:buffered-pin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:buffered-pin\">11</a> holds for these infinite local data. If every base vertex is unassigned, then $m_u=0$ on every bounded slab.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-slab-limits",
          "exact_quote": "On each bounded slab, the finite local functions $h_u,G_u,h_{u,i},h_{u,i}',R_{u,i}$, the probability and residue measures, and the data $m_u,\\ell_u,\\ell_{u,i}'$ have unique limits as finite graph balls increase to a component. They are measurable and independent of the exhaustion, and the local Cauchy-transform and Poisson-transform identities pass to the limits. Their supports have a common fixed-slab bound, and Equation <a href=\"#slab:local-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"slab:local-bounds\">[slab:local-bounds]</a> continues to hold.*\n\n*The Laurent coefficients through order $k$ of $h_u/z$, $zG_u$, $h_{u,i}/z$, $h_{u,i}'/z$, and $R_{u,i}$, and the moments of $\\gamma_u$ through order $k$, depend only on the data within graph distance $k$ of $u$. The same assertion holds for coefficients of logarithmic derivatives under data perturbations, with the local perturbation directions included in the data. It remains valid under arbitrary deletion configurations.*\n\n*At an unassigned vertex the buffered pin inequality of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:buffered-pin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:buffered-pin\">11</a> holds for these infinite local data. If every base vertex is unassigned, then $m_u=0$ on every bounded slab.",
          "source_id": "PM-300.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "On each bounded slab, the finite local functions $h_u,G_u,h_{u,i},h_{u,i}',R_{u,i}$, the probability and residue measures, and the data $m_u,\\ell_u,\\ell_{u,i}'$ have unique limits as finite graph balls increase to a component.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-far-deletion",
        "exact_quote": "Consider any bounded slab containing $V\\times[0,1]$, any base partial assignment, and any further measurable restriction that retains the central vertices being tested. Deleting a measurable set of vertices at heights $s>R$, or at heights $s<-R$, changes the tuple in Equation <a href=\"#slab:normalized-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"slab:normalized-data\">[slab:normalized-data]</a> by an amount tending to zero in $L^1(d\\mu\\,ds)$ on the central unassigned band as $R\\to\\infty$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-coherent-choice",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\tau_0>0$ there are arbitrarily large integers $L\\geq1$ with the following property, uniformly over all base partial assignments. On the slab $V\\times[0,L]$ one can choose a measurable prospective color $i(v)$ at each unassigned base vertex, using the same color at every height, so that $$\\frac1L\\int_{\\mathrm{unassigned}\\ \\mathrm{in}\\ V\\times[0,L]}\n       (\\ell_{(v,s),i(v)}'-\\ell_{(v,s)})\\,d\\widetilde\\mu(v,s)\n \\leq C\\Phi+\\tau_0,\n \\qquad\n \\Phi=\\frac1L\\int_{V\\times[0,L]}m_{(v,s)}\\,d\\widetilde\\mu(v,s).\n \\label{slab:coherent-inequality}$$ Here $C$ is the universal constant in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:buffered-pin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:buffered-pin\">11</a>, and every $\\ell_{u,i}'$ on the left is the insertion cost after changing only the lifted vertex $u$. The same $L$ can be used at every stage of a sequence of partial assignments.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-coherent-choice",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\tau_0>0$ there are arbitrarily large integers $L\\geq1$ with the following property, uniformly over all base partial assignments.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sparse-pinning",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\zeta,\\eta>0$ and $L_0\\geq1$, there are an integer $L\\geq L_0$ and a measurable partition $$V=\\mathcal U\\sqcup V_1\\sqcup\\cdots\\sqcup V_r$$ such that $\\mu(\\mathcal U)<\\eta$ and the following holds. On the surviving lifted vertices $(V\\setminus\\mathcal U)\\times[0,L]$, let $T_{\\mathrm{col}}^L$ be the restriction of $T$, keeping only entries between vertices whose basepoints belong to the same $V_i$, and put $$\\label{pin:surviving-operator}\n A_L=d^{1/2}(T_{\\mathrm{col}}^L-Kb\\mathbf 1)d^{1/2},\n \\qquad d_{(v,s)}=e^s.$$ Extend $m_u^{\\mathrm{surv}}=((A_L)_+)_{uu}$ by zero on the deleted vertices. Then $$\\label{pin:surviving-cost}\n \\frac1L\\int_{V\\times[0,L]}m_u^{\\mathrm{surv}}\\,\n d\\widetilde\\mu(u)<\\zeta.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sparse-pinning",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\zeta,\\eta>0$ and $L_0\\geq1$, there are an integer $L\\geq L_0$ and a measurable partition $$V=\\mathcal U\\sqcup V_1\\sqcup\\cdots\\sqcup V_r$$ such that $\\mu(\\mathcal U)<\\eta$ and the following holds. On the surviving lifted vertices $(V\\setminus\\mathcal U)\\times[0,L]$, let $T_{\\mathrm{col}}^L$ be the restriction of $T$, keeping only entries between vertices whose basepoints belong to the same $V_i$, and put $$\\label{pin:surviving-operator}\n A_L=d^{1/2}(T_{\\mathrm{col}}^L-Kb\\mathbf 1)d^{1/2},\n \\qquad d_{(v,s)}=e^s.$$ Extend $m_u^{\\mathrm{surv}}=((A_L)_+)_{uu}$ by zero on the deleted vertices. Then $$\\label{pin:surviving-cost}\n \\frac1L\\int_{V\\times[0,L]}m_u^{\\mathrm{surv}}\\,\n d\\widetilde\\mu(u)<\\zeta.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-300.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\sigma,\\beta>0$, and choose a real polynomial $p$ of degree at most $k\\geq1$ such that $$\\sup_{x\\in[-1,1]}\n \\left|p(x)-(x-(Kb+\\sigma))_+^2\\right|\\leq\\beta.$$ Suppose a base partial assignment, with unassigned set $\\mathcal U$, and a slab length $L\\geq\\max\\{1,4k\\log J\\}$ have surviving cost $$\\Psi=\\frac1L\\int_{V\\times[0,L]}m_u^{\\mathrm{surv}}\\,\n                         d\\widetilde\\mu(u),$$ where $m^{\\mathrm{surv}}$ is defined from <a href=\"#pin:surviving-operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pin:surviving-operator\">[pin:surviving-operator]</a>. Complete the base coloring by giving $\\mathcal U$ one additional color, and denote its paved matrix system by $T_{\\mathrm{pav}}$. Then $$\\label{pin:descent-bound}\n \\int_V\\bigl((T_{\\mathrm{pav}}-(Kb+\\sigma)\\mathbf 1)_+^2\\bigr)_{vv}\n                   \\,d\\mu(v)\n \\leq2\\beta+\\mu(\\mathcal U)+\\frac{2J^kN_k}{\\sigma}\\Psi.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-state-paving",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\gamma>0$ there is an integer $R_{\\mathrm{st}}(\\gamma)$ with the following property.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-random-freeness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal M$ have separable predual, let $\\phi_0$ be a faithful normal state, and let $D\\subset\\mathcal M_{\\phi_0}$ be a diffuse MASA. For every integer $n\\geq2$ there are unitaries $w_l\\in D$, with $w_l^n=1$, such that the joint star moments of $(w_l,U)$ converge to those of free unitaries $(w,U_{\\mathrm f})$, where $w$ is uniform on the $n$th roots of unity and $U_{\\mathrm f}$ is uniform on $\\{-1,1\\}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-no-central-functional",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subset P$ be a MASA such that no nonzero normal positive functional on $P$ centralizes $D$.         "source_id": "PM-300.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-300.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $D\\subset P$ be a MASA such that no nonzero normal positive functional on $P$ centralizes $D$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **MASA** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $A$ be a maximal abelian unital $*$-subalgebra, or MASA, of a complex von Neumann algebra $M$.
- **Partition compression** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite partition $\mathcal P=(p_i)_{i=1}^r$ of the unit by projections in $A$, write $$C_{\mathcal P}(z)=\sum_{i=1}^r p_i z p_i .$$
- **Norm paving** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Norm paving asks whether $C_{\mathcal P}(z)$ can be made close in operator norm to an element of $A$.
- **Approximation paving** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Approximation paving allows a bounded perturbation of $z$ within any prescribed strong-operator neighborhood before choosing the partition.
- **Partitions with zero projections** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Partitions are finite indexed families of orthogonal projections with sum $\mathbf 1$; we allow zero projections.
- **Centralizer of a state** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Here $$M_\varphi=\{a\in M:\varphi(az)=\varphi(za)\text{ for all }z\in M\}.$$
- **Clipping function** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): We use the same functional-calculus notation for continuous real functions, including $$\mathop{\mathrm{clip}}_c(t)=\max(-c,\min(t,c)),\qquad c>0.$$
- **Principal matrix system** ([source](#slab:edge-cocycle)): The system is *principal*: the indices are the points of an equivalence class, with one index for each point.
- **Maharam invariant measure** ([source](#slab:mass-transport)): The measure $$d\widetilde\mu(v,s)=d\mu(v)e^{-s}\,ds$$ is invariant along this relation.
- **Normalizer algebra** ([source](#pm-h-29)): Let $N$ be the von Neumann algebra generated by the groupoid normalizers of $A$ in $M$.
- **Groupoid normalizer** ([source](#pm-h-29)): These are the partial isometries $v$ with $v^*v,vv^*\in A$ such that conjugation by $v$ carries $A v^*v$ onto $A vv^*$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-partition-size-main | R(\varepsilon) | ≤ | $C_{\mathrm{ap}}\varepsilon^{-6}$ | Universal constant independent of algebra, operator and strong neighborhood. |
| q-approximant-norm | \left\lVert y\right\rVert | ≤ | $3\left\lVert x\right\rVert$ | Fixed constant, independent of the strong neighborhood. |
| q-relative-paving-error | \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}(y)-a\right\rVert | ≤ | $\varepsilon\left\lVert y\right\rVert$ | Error is relative to the approximant's own norm. |
| q-quadratic-expected | N(\varepsilon) | ≤ | $C\varepsilon^{-2}$ | Universal constant. |
| q-mss-largest-root | \max\operatorname{root}(f_Q) | ≤ | $2\sqrt{2/r}+2/r$ | Number of colors r. |
| q-residue-constant | C_0 | = | $6000$ | Universal; the value is stated to suffice. |
| q-normalizer-quotient-norm | \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}[Y]\right\rVert | ≤ | $6(Kb+\sigma)$ | Partition size (r+1)^2. |
| q-off-normalizer-norm | \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}[Z]\right\rVert | ≤ | $\frac4{\sqrt n}$ | Partition size n. |
| q-free-compression-norm | \left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^n p_j U_{\mathrm f}p_j\right\rVert | ≤ | $\frac2{\sqrt n}$ | Number of roots of unity n. |
| q-final-partition-count | R(\varepsilon) | = | $(r+1)^2n+m+1$ | r, n, m chosen as functions of epsilon and the buffer constant K. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-compression-net** [claim](#stmt-lem-compression-net) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compression-net): Schwarz inequality for unital completely positive maps and decreasing orthogonal projections on the state Hilbert space; cluster points lie in the MASA. -> Compressions converge to the conditional expectation in the sharp state seminorm.
- **step-sequence-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-sequence-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sequence-quotient): Ideal of state-null sequences with a multiplier algebra; centralizer property for MASA sequences. -> Quotient C*-algebra recording vanishing state errors with norm estimates.
- **step-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stability): Cycle expansion of determinants, normalized derivatives, Gauss-Lucas induction on half-plane nonvanishing. -> Stable expected polynomial with real-rooted specialization.
- **step-local-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-data): Pick-type quotient, Schur complement arrow matrix, inertia count for spectral shift, Poisson representation. -> Arrow representation, spectral measure, spectral shift and pin formulas.
- **step-mss-root** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-paving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-paving): Positive-defect completion, mixed characteristic polynomial bound, deletion of added Gram columns without raising the largest root. -> Largest-root bound for uniform random colorings.
- **step-residue-tails** [claim](#stmt-poly-tail-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-poly-tail-control): Markov inequality, imaginary part of Cauchy transform, Bessel inequality on arrow vectors. -> Near-mass and tail bounds on residues.
- **step-boole** [claim](#stmt-poly-boole) / [proof](#proof-stmt-poly-boole): Monotonicity between poles and comparison of top coefficients of the level equation. -> Exact length of exceedance sets.
- **step-buffered-pin** [claim](#stmt-lem-buffered-pin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-buffered-pin): Case split on the positive moment; Markov selection of a color; Boole length bounds and tail control. -> A buffered pin with controlled increase of exceedance length.
- **step-slab-limits** [claim](#stmt-lem-slab-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slab-limits): Formal Laurent locality, moment determinacy on compact support, weak-star convergence of spectral shifts. -> Measurable local limits on infinite components.
- **step-far-deletion** [claim](#stmt-lem-far-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-far-deletion): Logarithmic derivative bounds from stability, batched deletions via Borel colorings, normal families; edge interpolation for low deletions. -> Uniform integral comparison of normalized data under far deletions.
- **step-coherent-choice** [claim](#stmt-lem-coherent-choice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coherent-choice): Translation rule for normalized data, Fubini selection of a reference slice, comparison of unit bands. -> Height-independent color choice with a slab-average bound.
- **step-sparse-pinning** [claim](#stmt-prop-sparse-pinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sparse-pinning): Polynomial approximation of the positive part, sparse Bernoulli updates on a power of the base graph, iteration and Markov selection of a history, monotone deletion of unassigned vertices. -> Partial coloring with small unassigned measure and small surviving cost.
- **step-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent): Polynomial moment locality in finite balls, failure-set bound by mass transport. -> Base-level bound on the integrated spectral excess.
- **step-measured-paving** [claim](#stmt-thm-measured-paving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent-2): Choose tolerances, apply sparse pinning, add one color to the unassigned set, apply descent. -> Measurable coloring with small integrated squared excess.
- **step-normalizer-paving** [claim](#stmt-exp-normalizer-paving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-normalizer-paving): Kaplansky approximation by finite graph expressions, apply the measured theorem to both signs, common refinement. -> Quotient norm bound for the normalizer part.
- **step-atomless-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-atomless) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-atomless): Countable-section uniformization, polar decomposition, type I structure, contradiction with orthogonality to the normalizer space. -> Atomless kernel for operators orthogonal to the normalizer algebra.
- **step-random-freeness** [claim](#stmt-lem-random-freeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-random-freeness): Random labels on small cells, Haar compression, diffuse kernel return probabilities, analytic right multiplication for nontracial states, concentration. -> Asymptotic freeness of random diagonal unitaries.
- **step-free-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-compression): Vacuum projection estimate on reduced free-product words. -> Norm bound for the compressed free symmetry.
- **step-off-normalizer** [claim](#stmt-prop-off-normalizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-compression-2): Reduce to the diffuse summand, free-product two-by-two dilation, moment convergence and state-null excess. -> Quotient norm bound for the off-normalizer part.
- **step-state-paving** [claim](#stmt-thm-state-paving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-compression-3): Common refinement of both partition sequences and clipping of the block-diagonal residual. -> State-preserving paving with a state-null correction.
- **step-separable-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-separable-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separable-reduction): Countably generated submodel closed under the expectation and stored partitions. -> State paving without separability.
- **step-no-central-functional** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-central-functional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-central-functional): Random roots-of-unity unitary, near-orthogonal conjugated states, separating spectral projections, sequential effects and cyclic averaging. -> Positive contraction with large state mass and small cell compressions.
- **step-no-central-approximation** [claim](#stmt-gen-no-central-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gen-no-central-approximation): Apply the previous lemma to a state built from test vectors and expand the sandwich identity. -> Strong approximation with small paved norm.
- **step-support-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-gen-support-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gen-support-decomposition): Maximal orthogonal family under supports of centralizing functionals; glue corner expectations. -> Splitting into an expected corner and a corner without centralizing functionals.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gen-support-decomposition-2): Split the unit into three corners, pave each with the corresponding result, keep off-diagonal blocks, clip the diagonal witness. -> Approximation paving over an arbitrary MASA with a sixth-power count.
- **step-so-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-so-paving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gen-support-decomposition-3): Convert approximation paving to strong-operator paving using the normal expectation. -> Strong-operator paving with a partition size independent of the inclusion.
- **step-quadratic-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-quadratic-expected-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gen-support-decomposition-4): Invoke the companion quadratic strong-operator paving bound and convert it to approximation paving. -> Quadratic approximation paving for expected MASAs.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-partition-size**: For each epsilon in the open unit interval, the number of projections needed for approximation paving of a self-adjoint operator over any MASA of any complex von Neumann algebra is bounded by a universal constant times epsilon to the power minus six, independently of the algebra, the representation, the operator and the strong neighborhood.
- **inv-expected-quadratic-size**: When the MASA is the range of a normal conditional expectation, the partition size in the approximation-paving conclusion can be taken bounded by a universal constant times epsilon to the power minus two.
- **inv-initial-root-bound**: For a Hermitian contraction with zero diagonal on a finite index set, uniform random coloring with the stated number of colors gives an expected characteristic polynomial whose largest root obeys the stated explicit bound.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-unperturbed-operator-closeness**: Treat the paved approximant as uniformly close in operator norm to the original operator. -> The theorem only controls the approximant on a prescribed finite set of vectors; operator-norm closeness is not asserted, and the paving error is measured against the approximant's norm.
- **anti-quadratic-without-expectation**: Assume the quadratic partition count for an arbitrary MASA. -> The quadratic count is proved only when the MASA is the range of a normal conditional expectation; the general statement is the sixth-power one.
- **anti-strong-paving-without-expectation**: Apply the strong-operator paving corollary to an arbitrary MASA. -> The conversion to strong-operator paving of the original operator relies on a normal conditional expectation.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements about operators in von Neumann algebras with an unspecified universal constant, proved through measure-theoretic and operator-algebraic arguments (Maharam extensions, Borel colorings, free products, limits over nets of partitions). No finite procedure computes the objects asserted to exist, so no finite executable reference implementation with an independent oracle can be written.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence statement about von Neumann algebras and maximal abelian subalgebras. It gives no finite procedure that code could run, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamical system, physical model or discrete-time simulation appears. The objects are operators on an abstract Hilbert space. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result does not yield a checker, a solver or a user-facing utility. Its hypotheses require a complex von Neumann algebra and a maximal abelian subalgebra, which general tooling does not provide. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The partition-size bound is proved by measure-theoretic and probabilistic existence arguments (histories chosen by Markov's inequality, limits along nets). No explicit algorithm with stated complexity is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper defines no storage layout, index or container. Partitions of the unit in a maximal abelian subalgebra are abstract projections, not stored records. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No finite-dimensional kernel, circuit or numerical routine is described. The setting is infinite-dimensional operator algebras, so accelerator mapping has no basis in the source. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source contains no primitive, hardness assumption or security notion. Random diagonal phases appear only as a proof device for asymptotic freeness. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss or generalisation statement. The strong approximation of an operator on a finite vector set is a structural fact in operator algebras. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper minimises no objective and optimises no constants; it states that the sixth-power bound is not optimised. The positive-cost estimates serve only as proof tools. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No nodes, messages or fault model occur. The graph colourings are on equivalence relations of a probability space and decouple local updates inside the proof only. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is pure mathematics (operator algebras, mixed characteristic polynomials, measured equivalence relations). It supplies a proof ingredient for the paving conjecture, not an engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-masa-von-neumann-setting** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated for a complex von Neumann algebra acting on a Hilbert space, a MASA inside it, and a self-adjoint operator. Nothing is asserted for non-self-adjoint operators or for C*-algebras without a von Neumann envelope. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to non-self-adjoint operators', 'Applying the theorem to general C*-algebras without a concrete von Neumann representation']
- **limit-strong-approximant-only** (formal_scope): The paving is of a strong approximant y on a prescribed finite set of vectors, with a norm bound of three times the norm of x. Operator-norm closeness of y to x is not asserted, and the paving error is measured against the norm of y. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as norm paving of the original operator', 'Assuming uniform operator-norm approximation of x by y']
- **limit-sixth-power-nonoptimal** (approximation): The partition count is bounded by a universal constant times the inverse error to the sixth power; the constant is unspecified and the exponent is not optimized. No quadratic bound is claimed for the expectation-free setting. Blocked use: ['Quoting a quadratic partition count for an arbitrary MASA', 'Treating the sixth-power bound as sharp or as an explicit numerical size']
- **limit-expectation-needed-for-corollaries** (hypothesis): The strong-operator paving corollary and the quadratic corollary both require that the MASA be the range of a normal conditional expectation. Blocked use: ['Applying the strong-operator paving corollary to a MASA without a normal expectation', 'Using the quadratic count outside the expected case']
- **limit-state-paving-separable-faithful** (hypothesis): The state-preserving paving theorem is proved for a separable predual with a faithful normal state whose centralizer contains the MASA; the non-separable case is handled afterwards by a separate reduction. Blocked use: ['Using the state-preserving argument directly for a non-separable algebra without the reduction', 'Using it when the MASA is not in the centralizer of a faithful normal state']
- **limit-initial-root-bound-regime** (hypothesis): The initial largest-root bound holds for a Hermitian contraction with zero diagonal, zero penalties and equal color probabilities; it is not claimed for general penalties or probabilities. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to matrices with nonzero diagonal', 'Applying it to nonuniform color probabilities']
- **limit-measured-paving-bounded-degree** (hypothesis): The measured paving theorem needs the graph support of bounded degree and bounded cocycle ratios on edges, and a number of colors satisfying the strict root inequality. Blocked use: ['Applying it to unbounded-degree graph supports', 'Choosing too few colors so that the strict inequality fails']
- **limit-nonconstructive-selection** (nonconstructive): The sparse-pinning partition and the coherent slab choice are selected by expectation and averaging arguments, so the paving is obtained by existence; no procedure computing it is given. Blocked use: ['Treating the construction as a runnable algorithm', 'Quoting a running time for finding the partition']
- **limit-no-central-functional-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The corner argument for small compressions applies only when no nonzero normal positive functional centralizes the MASA; otherwise the support decomposition must first split the unit. Blocked use: ['Applying the small-compression lemma to the corner carrying centralizing functionals']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the approximation-paving conjecture of Popa and Vaes for every maximal abelian subalgebra of a complex von Neumann algebra. For each $`0\lt \varepsilon\lt 1`$, every self-adjoint operator is a strong limit of self-adjoint operators of norm at most three times its norm, each admitting a norm paving with error at most <i>ε</i> times the approximant's own norm. The number of projections is at most $`C\varepsilon^{-6}`$ for a universal constant <i>C</i>. No separability or conditional-expectation hypothesis is required.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $A$ be a maximal abelian unital $*$-subalgebra, or MASA, of a complex von Neumann algebra $M$. For a finite partition $\mathcal P=(p_i)_{i=1}^r$ of the unit by projections in $A$, write $$C_{\mathcal P}(z)=\sum_{i=1}^r p_i z p_i .$$ Norm paving asks whether $C_{\mathcal P}(z)$ can be made close in operator norm to an element of $A$. Approximation paving allows a bounded perturbation of $z$ within any prescribed strong-operator neighborhood before choosing the partition.

Kadison and Singer posed the pure-state extension problem for the atomic diagonal MASA of $\mathcal B(\ell^2)$ <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KS59](#ref-KS59)]. Anderson formulated its diagonal paving counterpart <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Anderson79](#ref-Anderson79)], and Marcus, Spielman and Srivastava resolved it by mixed characteristic polynomials <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[MSS15](#ref-MSS15)]. For general MASAs, ordinary norm paving of the original operator is too restrictive: in the separable-predual setting, it characterizes type I algebras with a normal expectation onto the MASA <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Theorem 3.3]. Popa and Vaes introduced strong-operator and approximation versions of paving and conjectured that every MASA has both properties <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Definitions 2.2–2.3 and Conjecture 2.8(1)]. We resolve the approximation-paving assertion of that conjecture positively.

Popa proved norm paving in ultraproducts of singular finite MASAs, and hence strong-operator paving in the original finite inclusions <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Popa14](#ref-Popa14)]. Popa and Vaes established strong-operator and approximation paving for type I algebras with separable predual and for Cartan inclusions in amenable algebras or arising from free ergodic profinite actions <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Sections 3–4]. For singular finite MASAs they obtained quadratic paving bounds <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Theorem 5.1], later sharpened in <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[PV16](#ref-PV16)]. These restricted cases do not provide the expectation-free approximation theorem below.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There is a universal constant $C_{\mathrm{ap}}>0$ such that for every $0<\varepsilon<1$ there is an integer $R(\varepsilon)\leq C_{\mathrm{ap}}\varepsilon^{-6}$ with the following property. Let $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a complex von Neumann algebra, let $A\subseteq M$ be a MASA, and let $x=x^*\in M$. For every finite $F\subset H$ and every $\delta>0$, there exist $y=y^*\in M$, a partition $\mathcal P=(p_i)_{i=1}^{R(\varepsilon)}$ of the unit in $A$, and $a=a^*\in A$, such that $$\left\lVert y\right\rVert\leq3\left\lVert x\right\rVert,\qquad
 \left\lVert(y-x)\xi\right\rVert<\delta\quad(\xi\in F),$$ and $$\left\lVert a\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert y\right\rVert,\qquad
 \left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}(y)-a\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon\left\lVert y\right\rVert.$$ Zero projections may be included in the partition.*

</div>

Thus the norm-control constant is universal, and the number of projections is independent of the algebra, its representation, the operator, and the strong neighborhood. We do not optimize the sixth-power bound. The relative error in the theorem is measured against the actual approximant’s norm; a norming test vector and a final clipping argument will ensure that normalization.

There is also a direct consequence for strong-operator paving when a normal expectation exists. In its definition, the compression is estimated on a large projection whose complement lies in a prescribed strong neighborhood of zero.

<a id="stmt-cor-so-paving"></a>
<div id="cor:so-paving" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Suppose that a MASA $A\subseteq M$ is the range of a normal conditional expectation. For each $0<\varepsilon<1$ there is an integer $R_s(\varepsilon)$, independent of the inclusion, such that for every $x=x^*\in M$ and every strong-operator neighborhood $\mathcal V$ of zero, there exist an $R_s(\varepsilon)$-partition $\mathcal P$ in $A$, an $a=a^*\in A$ with $\left\lVert a\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert$, and a projection $q\in M$ with $\mathbf 1-q\in\mathcal V$, satisfying $$\left\lVert q(C_{\mathcal P}(x)-a)q\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon\left\lVert x\right\rVert.$$*

</div>

The proof is given in Section <a href="#gen:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:consequences">7.4</a>.

The separate companion <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[OAQuadratic26](#ref-OAQuadratic26), Theorem 1.1] proves a direct quadratic strong-operator paving bound for the original operator. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> instead norm-paves a nearby bounded operator, with error relative to that approximant’s norm. The two main proofs are independent; the conversion in Corollary <a href="#cor:so-paving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:so-paving">2</a> retains its normal-expectation hypothesis. We claim no quadratic bound for $R(\varepsilon)$.

<a id="stmt-cor-quadratic-expected-approximation"></a>
<div id="cor:quadratic-expected-approximation" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Quadratic approximation paving for expected MASAs). *There is a universal constant $C>0$ such that for every $0<\varepsilon<1$ one can choose an integer $N(\varepsilon)\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}$ with the following property. If the MASA $A\subseteq M$ is the range of a normal conditional expectation, then the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> holds with $N(\varepsilon)$ in place of $R(\varepsilon)$.*

</div>

The proof is given in Section <a href="#gen:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:consequences">7.4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### The measured argument.

The first part of the proof develops a measurable version of one-sided matrix paving for nonsingular equivalence relations. The finite paving-polynomial framework of <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[RL20](#ref-RL20), [RS21](#ref-RS21)] supplies the starting point. Its stable expected determinants yield local spectral probabilities and spectral-shift functions. A buffered pinning lemma selects a color while increasing the diagonal penalty by a fixed universal factor and controlling the increase of positive-root mass. The initial root estimate from <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[RS21](#ref-RS21), Lemma 24 and proof of Theorem 25] is derived here from <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[MSS15](#ref-MSS15), Theorems 4.1 and 5.1], using positive-defect completion and deletion of added Gram columns.

Nonsingularity is handled by passing to the Maharam extension, where mass transport is invariant, and weighting the matrix by the height variable. Uniform comparisons between slabs allow the pinning decisions to descend to a single coloring of the base. Sparse random updates then control the accumulated cost. Only local independence is needed; auxiliary graph colorings do not increase the number of colors in the final paving. The slab comparison and buffered-pinning estimates isolate the two uniformity issues in this passage from finite matrices to measurable operators.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The operator-algebra argument.

For a MASA in the centralizer of a faithful normal state, we first pave the algebra generated by its normalizers, using the Feldman–Moore representation <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[FM77](#ref-FM77)] and the measured theorem. The complementary part has diffuse left–right kernels. We adapt the free-product matrix dilation of <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), proof of Theorem 5.1], using random diagonal phases to obtain asymptotic freeness with respect to the induced state. The proof controls the moments and their concentration without assuming traciality, using analytic right multiplication for the modular group.

Finally, a separable reduction preserves the partition bounds. The corner carrying centralizing normal positive functionals has a normal expectation; on the complementary corner, almost disjoint conjugate states produce positive contractions with small diagonal compressions. A three-corner construction preserves the original off-diagonal entries until the final partition is chosen. This is the step that removes the normal-expectation hypothesis.

All new estimates needed for these passages are proved below. We use established structure theorems for conditional expectations, standard form, type I algebras and free products, with their hypotheses specified at the points of application.

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes the state-topology tools; Sections <a href="#poly:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="poly:section">3</a>–<a href="#sec:pinning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pinning">5</a> prove the measured paving theorem, Section <a href="#sec:expected" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:expected">6</a> handles the state-preserving case, and Section <a href="#gen:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gen:section">7</a> removes the remaining hypotheses.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### State topology and bounded sequence quotients

All algebras and Hilbert spaces are over $\mathbb C$. A MASA is automatically a von Neumann algebra: its relative commutant equals itself, by taking real and imaginary parts. Partitions are finite indexed families of orthogonal projections with sum $\mathbf 1$; we allow zero projections. For a self-adjoint operator $t$, let $t_+$ denote its positive part. We use the same functional-calculus notation for continuous real functions, including $$\mathop{\mathrm{clip}}_c(t)=\max(-c,\min(t,c)),\qquad c>0.$$

Suppose for this section that $M$ has a faithful normal state $\varphi$, and $A\subseteq M_\varphi$ is a MASA in its centralizer. Here $$M_\varphi=\{a\in M:\varphi(az)=\varphi(za)\text{ for all }z\in M\}.$$ The modular group fixes $A$ pointwise. The conditional-expectation theorem gives a faithful normal $\varphi$-preserving expectation $E_A:M\to A$; see <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Takesaki72](#ref-Takesaki72)] or <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Hiai20](#ref-Hiai20), Theorem 6.6]. Set $$\left\lVert z\right\rVert_\varphi=\varphi(z^*z)^{1/2},
 \qquad
 \left\lVert z\right\rVert_\varphi^\#=\left\lVert z\right\rVert_\varphi+\left\lVert z^*\right\rVert_\varphi.$$ On bounded sets these seminorms determine, respectively, the $\sigma$-strong and $\sigma$-strong\* topologies. Indeed, in the faithful state representation, $\left\lVert z\right\rVert_\varphi=\left\lVert z\xi_\varphi\right\rVert$, and $M'\xi_\varphi$ is dense. Boundedness therefore promotes convergence on $\xi_\varphi$ to strong convergence on all vectors. The bounded $\sigma$-strong topology is intrinsic to the von Neumann algebra. Apply the same reasoning to adjoints for the second assertion. In standard $L^2(M)$ notation we write $\xi_\varphi=\varphi^{1/2}$. Elements of $A$ commute with this vector under the left and right actions.

<a id="stmt-lem-compression-net"></a>
<div id="lem:compression-net" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Every $A$-partition compression $C_{\mathcal Q}$ preserves $\varphi$ and is contractive for both state seminorms. As $\mathcal Q$ refines through all finite $A$-partitions, $$C_{\mathcal Q}(z)\longrightarrow E_A(z)
 \quad\text{in }\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_\varphi^\#
 \qquad(z\in M).$$ The same holds along any refining sequence of partitions generating $A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-compression-net"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-compression-net</summary>

*Proof.* The centralizer identity gives $\varphi(C_{\mathcal Q}(z))=\varphi(z)$. Compression is unital completely positive, so its Schwarz inequality gives state-norm contraction; apply it to adjoints as well. On the state Hilbert space the compression is implemented by the orthogonal projection $$\zeta\longmapsto\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}q\zeta q.$$ These projections decrease under refinement. Their action on $z\varphi^{1/2}$ is $C_{\mathcal Q}(z)\varphi^{1/2}$. Meanwhile the operators $C_{\mathcal Q}(z)$ are uniformly bounded. Every ultraweak cluster point commutes with all projections of $A$, and hence is in $A$. Since $E_A C_{\mathcal Q}(z)=E_A(z)$, the cluster point equals $E_A(z)$. The Hilbert-space limit of the decreasing projections must therefore be $E_A(z)\varphi^{1/2}$. Repeating for $z^*$ proves the claim. For a generating refining sequence, commuting with all of its partition projections is already equivalent to commuting with $A$, so the same proof applies. ◻

</details>

The following quotient allows us to record vanishing state errors while retaining norm estimates. Its multiplier condition matters when $\varphi$ is not tracial.

<a id="stmt-lem-sequence-quotient"></a>
<div id="lem:sequence-quotient" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *Let $$\mathcal I_\varphi=
 \{(z_j)\in\ell^\infty(M):\left\lVert z_j\right\rVert_\varphi^\#\to0\},$$ and let $$\mathcal C_\varphi=
 \{t\in\ell^\infty(M):
 t\mathcal I_\varphi\subseteq\mathcal I_\varphi,\
 \mathcal I_\varphi t\subseteq\mathcal I_\varphi\}.$$ Then $\mathcal C_\varphi$ is a unital $C^*$-algebra and $\mathcal I_\varphi$ is a closed two-sided ideal in it. Constant sequences from $M$ and all sequences in $\ell^\infty(A)$ belong to $\mathcal C_\varphi$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-sequence-quotient"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-sequence-quotient</summary>

*Proof.* The set $\mathcal I_\varphi$ is self-adjoint and norm closed. If $s,t\in\mathcal I_\varphi$, then $$\left\lVert s_jt_j\right\rVert_\varphi\leq
 \left\lVert s_j\right\rVert\left\lVert t_j\right\rVert_\varphi,\qquad
 \left\lVert(s_jt_j)^*\right\rVert_\varphi\leq
 \left\lVert t_j\right\rVert\left\lVert s_j^*\right\rVert_\varphi ,$$ so $st\in\mathcal I_\varphi$. The two multiplier conditions now show directly that $\mathcal C_\varphi$ is a unital, norm-closed $*$-algebra, contains $\mathcal I_\varphi$, and makes it an ideal. Multiplication by a fixed bounded operator is continuous on bounded sets for strong\* convergence, so constant sequences are multipliers.

If $a\in A$, then left multiplication has state-norm bound $\left\lVert a\right\rVert$, and the centralizer property gives $$\left\lVert ta\right\rVert_\varphi^2
 =\varphi(a^*t^*ta)
 =\varphi(t^*t aa^*)
 \leq \left\lVert a\right\rVert^2\varphi(t^*t).$$ The last inequality follows from positivity, since $\varphi(t^*t b)=\varphi(b^{1/2}t^*t b^{1/2})\geq0$ for $b\geq0$ in $A$. The same estimates apply to adjoints, uniformly on bounded $A$-sequences. Thus these sequences are multipliers. ◻

</details>

Write $[t_j]$ for an image in $\mathcal C_\varphi/\mathcal I_\varphi$. A sequence of $A$-partitions of uniformly bounded size acts by compression in this quotient. If $q$ refines $p$, its compression applied to a $p$-compression is its compression of the original element. Since these maps are unital and positive, a common refinement preserves separate scalar upper and lower bounds for a self-adjoint element. In particular it preserves a norm bound.
