Use the projections of $\mathcal P_f$, the projections of $\mathcal P_h$, and the single projection $g$ as distinct members of one $A$-partition $\mathcal P$, padding with zeros in absent corners. It has the size in Equation <a href="#gen:final-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:final-size">[gen:final-size]</a> and kills every off-diagonal block between $f,g,h$. With $d=d_f$ on $f$ and zero on $g+h$, we obtain $$d=d^*\in A,\qquad\left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}(y)-d\right\rVert\leq\gamma.$$ Since compression is norm-contractive, $\left\lVert d\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert y\right\rVert+\gamma$. Clip $d$ by functional calculus to the interval $[-\left\lVert y\right\rVert,\left\lVert y\right\rVert]$, obtaining $a\in A$ with $\left\lVert a\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert y\right\rVert$ and $\left\lVert a-d\right\rVert\leq\gamma$. It follows that $$\left\lVert C_{\mathcal P}(y)-a\right\rVert
 \leq2\gamma=\varepsilon/2\leq\varepsilon\left\lVert y\right\rVert.$$ Equations <a href="#gen:norm-control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:norm-control">[gen:norm-control]</a> and <a href="#gen:test-control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="gen:test-control">[gen:test-control]</a> give the remaining requirements. The partition size depends only on $\varepsilon$, since the bound in Theorem <a href="#thm:state-paving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:state-paving">18</a> is uniform over the state-preserving inclusions used here. Finally, scaling $y$ and $a$ by the original $\left\lVert x\right\rVert$, and first dividing the requested strong tolerance by that norm, proves the theorem with the universal constant $\kappa=3$. ◻

</details>

<a id="gen:consequences"></a>
#### Consequences for related paving formulations

The main theorem concerns norm paving of bounded approximants. The two introductory consequences use the normal-expectation hypothesis to pass between that formulation and strong-operator paving of the original operator. The quadratic consequence additionally uses the separate companion theorem.

<details id="proof-stmt-gen-support-decomposition-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-gen-support-decomposition</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:so-paving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:so-paving">2</a>.* By Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, every such $x$ is $(\varepsilon/12,R(\varepsilon/12);3)$-approximately pavable in the terminology of <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Definition 2.3]. Apply <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Proposition 2.4(1)] with $\varepsilon'=\varepsilon/6$. Its error is $2\cdot3\cdot(\varepsilon/6)=\varepsilon$, so $R_s(\varepsilon)=R(\varepsilon/12)$ suffices. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-gen-support-decomposition-4"><summary>Proof of stmt-gen-support-decomposition</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:quadratic-expected-approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:quadratic-expected-approximation">3</a>.* By <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[OAQuadratic26](#ref-OAQuadratic26), Theorem 1.1], every self-adjoint $x\in M$ is $(\varepsilon/2,r_{\varepsilon/2})$ so-pavable, where $r_{\varepsilon/2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}$ for a universal $C$. Since $\varepsilon/2<\varepsilon$, <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Proposition 2.4(2)] makes $x$ $(\varepsilon,r_{\varepsilon/2};3)$-approximately pavable. Its proof supplies self-adjoint approximants and diagonal witnesses, with paving error relative to the approximant’s norm. The strong-limit net in <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[PV15](#ref-PV15), Definition 2.3] supplies the finite-vector conclusion with the fixed count $N(\varepsilon)=r_{\varepsilon/2}$. ◻

</details>

      "caption": "Height geometry behind Lemmas 14 and 15 (not to scale). High and low deletions are controlled by different estimates. Translated interior unit bands compare with the reference slab in integrated normalized data; the boundary bands have total length at most $2R + 2$. The colored vertical lines are base fibers ${v} × [0,L]$, not lifted relation orbits.",

\\definecolor{mahblue}{RGB}{39,91,134}\n  \\definecolor{mahgold}{RGB}{149,100,27}\n  \\definecolor{mahgreen}{RGB}{39,116,86}\n\n  \\node[anchor=west,font=\\small\\bfseries] at (0,5.7)\n    {(a) Far \\draw[heightline] (.8,.4) rectangle (2.7,5.05);\n  \\draw[thick] (.8,1.2) rectangle (2.7,4.25);\n  \\foreach \\y/\\t in {1.2/{-R},2.5/{0},3.15/{1},4.25/{R}} {\n    \\draw[heightline] (.68,\\y)--(2.7,\\y);\n    \\node[anchor=east] at (.62,\\y) {$\\t$};\n  }\n  \\draw[dashed,mahgreen] (.8,2.82)--(2.7,2.82);\n  \\node[anchor=west,align=left,text=mahgold] at (2.92,4.65)\n    {High deletion: small measure\\\\$\\widetilde\\mu(D)\\leq e^{-R}$};\n  \\node[anchor=west,align=left] at (2.92,2.82)\n    {Test band $[0,1]$\\\\reference slice $s_0$};\n  \\node[anchor=west,align=left,text=mahblue] at (2.92,.78)\n    {Low deletion: small cut matrix\\\\$\\|\\Delta W\\|=O(e^{-R})$};\n  \\node[align=center] at (1.75,-.1)\n    {Retained reference slab\\\\$V\\times[-R,R]$};\n\n  \\node[anchor=west,font=\\small\\bfseries] at (8,5.7)\n    {(b) One base color at every height};\n  \\fill[gray!13] (8.65,.4) rectangle (11.4,1.12);\n  \\fill[gray!13] (8.65,4.33) rectangle (11.4,5.05);\n  \\fill[mahgreen!7] (8.65,1.12) rectangle (11.4,4.33);\n  \\draw[heightline] (8.65,.4) rectangle (11.4,5.05);\n  \\draw[heightline,dashed] (8.65,1.12)--(11.4,1.12);\n  \\draw[heightline,dashed] (8.65,4.33)--(11.4,4.33);\n  \\draw[mahgreen] (8.65,2.43) rectangle (11.4,3.05);\n  \\foreach \\x/\\c in {9.13/mahblue,10.02/mahgold,10.91/mahgreen} {\n    \\draw[fiber,\\c] (\\x,.54)--(\\x,4.91);\n    \\foreach \\y in {.75,1.65,2.74,3.65,4.7}\n      \\fill[\\c] (\\x,\\y) circle (1.8pt);\n  }\n  \\node[anchor=east] at (8.48,.4) {$0$};\n  \\node[anchor=east] at (8.48,5.05) {$L$};\n  \\node[anchor=west,align=left] at (11.58,4.65) {Boundary\\\\bands};\n  \\node[anchor=west,align=left] at (11.58,2.74)\n    {Interior unit band\\\\$[k,k+1]$};\n  \\node[anchor=west,align=left] at (11.58,.76) {Boundary\\\\bands};\n  \\node at (9.13,.08) {$v_1$};\n  \\node at (10.02,.08) {$v_2$};\n  \\node at (10.91,.08) {$v_3$};\n  \\node[align=center] at (10.02,-.52)\n    {Long slab $V\\times[0,L]$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Two schematic diagrams illustrating height geometry:\n- Subfigure (a) \"Far deletions around a test band\": A vertical column with a vertical axis labeled with ticks $-R$, $0$, $1$, and $R$. The region above $R$ is shaded tan and labeled \"High deletion: small measure $\\widetilde{\\mu}(\\mathcal{D}) \\le e^{-R}$\". The region between $0$ and $1$ is shaded light blue and labeled \"Test band $[0, 1]$\" and \"reference slice $s_0$\". The region below $-R$ is shaded tan and labeled \"Low deletion: small cut matrix $\\|\\Delta W\\| = O(e^{-R})$\". A vertical bracket spanning from $-R$ to $R$ on the left is labeled \"Retained reference slab $V \\times [-R, R]$\".\n- Subfigure (b) \"One base color at every height\": A vertical slab labeled at the bottom as \"Long slab $V \\times [0, L]$\" with a vertical height axis running from $0$ at the bottom to $L$ at the top. Three vertical colored lines extend from height $0$ to $L$, labeled at the bottom with base vertices $v_1$ (blue), $v_2$ (orange), and $v_3$ (green). Shaded horizontal bands at the top and bottom are labeled \"Boundary bands\". An interior horizontal segment between heights $k$ and $k+1$ is bounded by double arrows and labeled \"Interior unit band $[k, k + 1]$\"."
    }
